FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\capability_optimisation_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center capability optimisation service.

Provides provider recommendations per AI capability.

Architecture:

Provider Usage
        |
        v
Provider Comparison Analytics
        |
        v
CapabilityOptimisationService
        |
        v
Capability Optimisation Recommendation
        |
        v
Human Review


Responsibilities:

- evaluate provider options for a capability;
- compare operational evidence;
- identify the current provider;
- identify the recommended provider/model;
- explain the recommendation;
- calculate recommendation confidence.


Does NOT:

- change Runtime routing;
- execute providers;
- approve recommendations;
- send emails;
- perform autonomous optimisation.

Provider changes remain human-approved.

Hackathon scoring:

The initial optimisation score considers:

- success rate;
- latency;
- cost.

Quality remains available in the provider usage model and
can be incorporated into the scoring policy without changing
the recommendation contract.

Current Hackathon weighting:

- success rate: 50%;
- latency: 40%;
- cost: 10%.

The weighting intentionally prioritises learner-facing
operational reliability and responsiveness over pure
cost minimisation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from decimal import Decimal

from app.application.provider_management.provider_comparison_service import (
    ProviderComparison,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityOptimisationRecommendation:
    """
    Advisory optimisation recommendation for one
    AI capability.
    """

    capability: str

    current_provider: str

    current_model: str

    recommended_provider: str

    recommended_model: str

    reason: str

    confidence: float


class CapabilityOptimisationService:
    """
    Generates capability-specific provider recommendations.

    Recommendations are derived from provider comparison
    evidence rather than from hard-coded provider preferences.
    """

    def recommend(
        self,
        *,
        capability: str,
        current_provider: str,
        current_model: str,
        recommended_provider: str,
        recommended_model: str,
        reason: str,
        confidence: float,
    ) -> CapabilityOptimisationRecommendation:
        """
        Create an advisory capability optimisation
        recommendation.

        This method remains available as a low-level
        construction contract for callers that already
        have a validated recommendation.
        """

        self._validate_identity(
            capability=capability,
            current_provider=current_provider,
            current_model=current_model,
            recommended_provider=recommended_provider,
            recommended_model=recommended_model,
        )

        if not reason.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Recommendation reason cannot be empty."
            )

        self._validate_confidence(
            confidence
        )

        return CapabilityOptimisationRecommendation(
            capability=capability,
            current_provider=current_provider,
            current_model=current_model,
            recommended_provider=recommended_provider,
            recommended_model=recommended_model,
            reason=reason,
            confidence=confidence,
        )

    def recommend_from_comparisons(
        self,
        *,
        capability: str,
        current_provider: str,
        current_model: str,
        comparisons: tuple[
            ProviderComparison,
            ...,
        ],
    ) -> CapabilityOptimisationRecommendation:
        """
        Generate an advisory recommendation from
        provider comparison analytics.

        Only comparisons for the requested capability
        are considered.

        The service does not change Runtime routing.

        The recommendation considers:

        - success rate;
        - latency;
        - cost.
        """

        if not capability.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Capability cannot be empty."
            )

        if not current_provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Current provider cannot be empty."
            )

        if not current_model.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Current model cannot be empty."
            )

        if comparisons is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider comparisons cannot be None."
            )

        capability_comparisons = tuple(
            comparison
            for comparison in comparisons
            if comparison.capability == capability
        )

        if not capability_comparisons:
            raise ValueError(
                "No provider comparison data available "
                f"for capability '{capability}'."
            )

        scored = [
            (
                comparison,
                self._calculate_score(
                    comparison,
                    capability_comparisons,
                ),
            )
            for comparison in capability_comparisons
        ]

        recommended_comparison, best_score = max(
            scored,
            key=lambda item: item[1],
        )

        confidence = self._calculate_confidence(
            best_score=best_score,
            comparisons=scored,
        )

        reason = self._build_reason(
            comparison=recommended_comparison,
            current_provider=current_provider,
            current_model=current_model,
        )

        return CapabilityOptimisationRecommendation(
            capability=capability,
            current_provider=current_provider,
            current_model=current_model,
            recommended_provider=(
                recommended_comparison.provider
            ),
            recommended_model=(
                recommended_comparison.model
            ),
            reason=reason,
            confidence=confidence,
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _calculate_score(
        comparison: ProviderComparison,
        comparisons: tuple[
            ProviderComparison,
            ...,
        ],
    ) -> float:
        """
        Calculate a deterministic operational score.

        Current Hackathon weighting:

        - success rate: 50%;
        - latency: 40%;
        - cost: 10%.

        The weighting intentionally prioritises
        learner-facing operational reliability and
        responsiveness over pure cost minimisation.
        """

        success_score = (
            comparison.success_rate / 100.0
        )

        max_latency = max(
            item.average_latency_ms
            for item in comparisons
        )

        max_cost = max(
            item.average_cost_per_request
            for item in comparisons
        )

        if max_latency == 0:
            latency_score = 1.0
        else:
            latency_score = (
                1.0
                - (
                    comparison.average_latency_ms
                    / max_latency
                )
            )

        if max_cost == Decimal("0"):
            cost_score = 1.0
        else:
            cost_score = float(
                Decimal("1")
                - (
                    comparison.average_cost_per_request
                    / max_cost
                )
            )

        return (
            (success_score * 0.50)
            + (latency_score * 0.40)
            + (cost_score * 0.10)
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _calculate_confidence(
        *,
        best_score: float,
        comparisons: list[
            tuple[
                ProviderComparison,
                float,
            ]
        ],
    ) -> float:
        """
        Convert comparative evidence into a bounded
        recommendation confidence.

        Confidence represents comparative evidence,
        not certainty about future provider behaviour.
        """

        if len(comparisons) == 1:
            return round(
                min(
                    100.0,
                    max(
                        0.0,
                        best_score * 100.0,
                    ),
                ),
                2,
            )

        sorted_scores = sorted(
            (
                score
                for _, score in comparisons
            ),
            reverse=True,
        )

        second_score = sorted_scores[1]

        separation = (
            best_score - second_score
        )

        confidence = (
            60.0
            + (separation * 100.0)
        )

        return round(
            min(
                99.0,
                max(
                    0.0,
                    confidence,
                ),
            ),
            2,
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _build_reason(
        *,
        comparison: ProviderComparison,
        current_provider: str,
        current_model: str,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Produce a human-readable explanation suitable
        for the Operations Center.
        """

        if (
            comparison.provider == current_provider
            and comparison.model == current_model
        ):
            return (
                "Current provider remains the strongest "
                "observed option for this capability based "
                "on success rate, latency and cost."
            )

        return (
            f"{comparison.provider} / "
            f"{comparison.model} currently provides "
            f"the strongest observed operational profile "
            f"for this capability based on success rate, "
            f"latency and cost."
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_identity(
        *,
        capability: str,
        current_provider: str,
        current_model: str,
        recommended_provider: str,
        recommended_model: str,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Validate provider recommendation identity fields.
        """

        if not capability.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Capability cannot be empty."
            )

        if not current_provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Current provider cannot be empty."
            )

        if not current_model.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Current model cannot be empty."
            )

        if not recommended_provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Recommended provider cannot be empty."
            )

        if not recommended_model.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Recommended model cannot be empty."
            )

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_confidence(
        confidence: float,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Validate recommendation confidence.
        """

        if not 0.0 <= confidence <= 100.0:
            raise ValueError(
                "Confidence must be between 0 and 100."
            )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\email_notification_adapter.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
AI Service Provider Manager email notification adapter.

Provides the notification boundary for provider
operational escalations.

Architecture:

EscalationService
        |
        v
EmailNotificationAdapter
        |
        v
Email Infrastructure


Responsibilities:

- translate escalation information into an email;
- prepare human-readable notification content;
- delegate delivery to an email sender.

Does NOT:

- create escalations;
- decide severity;
- approve recommendations;
- change provider routing;
- execute agents;
- modify Runtime configuration.

The adapter is deliberately provider-independent.

A concrete email implementation can later use:

- Resend;
- SMTP;
- another approved email provider.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    ProviderEscalation,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EscalationEmail:
    """
    Prepared escalation email.

    This is a transport-independent email contract.
    """

    recipient: str

    subject: str

    body: str


class EmailNotificationAdapter(ABC):
    """
    Notification boundary for escalation emails.

    Application logic depends on this abstraction,
    not on a concrete email provider.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def prepare_escalation_email(
        self,
        *,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
        recipient: str,
    ) -> EscalationEmail:
        """
        Prepare an escalation notification.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Email preparation must be implemented."
        )


class InMemoryEmailNotificationAdapter(
    EmailNotificationAdapter,
):
    """
    Development and testing email adapter.

    No real email is sent.

    Prepared emails are retained so tests and
    demonstrations can inspect the notification.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        self._emails: list[
            EscalationEmail
        ] = []

    def prepare_escalation_email(
        self,
        *,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
        recipient: str,
    ) -> EscalationEmail:
        """
        Prepare and retain an escalation email.

        No external email service is contacted.
        """

        if escalation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation cannot be None."
            )

        if not recipient.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Email recipient cannot be empty."
            )

        subject = (
            "EasyChatBot AI Operations "
            f"Escalation: "
            f"{escalation.severity.value.upper()}"
        )

        recommendation = (
            escalation.recommendation
            or "No recommendation provided."
        )

        body = (
            "EasyChatBot AI Operations Center\n"
            "\n"
            f"Escalation ID: "
            f"{escalation.escalation_id}\n"
            f"Type: "
            f"{escalation.escalation_type.value}\n"
            f"Severity: "
            f"{escalation.severity.value}\n"
            f"Provider: "
            f"{escalation.provider}\n"
            f"Capability: "
            f"{escalation.capability}\n"
            f"Detected: "
            f"{escalation.created_at.isoformat()}\n"
            "\n"
            "Issue\n"
            "-----\n"
            f"{escalation.message}\n"
            "\n"
            "AI Recommendation\n"
            "-----------------\n"
            f"{recommendation}\n"
            "\n"
            "Human review is required before "
            "any operational change."
        )

        email = EscalationEmail(
            recipient=recipient,
            subject=subject,
            body=body,
        )

        self._emails.append(
            email
        )

        return email

    def get_prepared_emails(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationEmail]:
        """
        Return prepared emails for testing or inspection.
        """

        return list(
            self._emails
        )

    def clear(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Clear prepared notifications.
        """

        self._emails.clear()

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_detail_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation detail service.

Provides the read-only application contract used by
the Escalation Detail View.

Architecture:

Escalation Repository
        |
        v
EscalationDetailService
        |
        v
EscalationDetail
        |
        v
AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- retrieve one escalation;
- expose a presentation-independent detail contract;
- preserve the escalation domain information.

Does NOT:

- modify escalations;
- resolve escalations;
- approve recommendations;
- send emails;
- change provider routing;
- execute providers.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_repository import (
    EscalationRepository,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    EscalationSeverity,
    EscalationStatus,
    EscalationType,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EscalationDetail:
    """
    Read-only detail representation of one escalation.
    """

    escalation_id: str

    escalation_type: EscalationType

    severity: EscalationSeverity

    provider: str

    capability: str

    message: str

    recommendation: str | None

    created_at: datetime

    status: EscalationStatus


class EscalationDetailService:
    """
    Provides read-only access to escalation details.

    The repository remains the source of truth.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        repository: EscalationRepository,
    ) -> None:

        if repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation repository cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository = repository

    def get_detail(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationDetail | None:
        """
        Retrieve one escalation as a detail contract.

        Returns None when the escalation does not exist.
        """

        if not escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation id cannot be empty."
            )

        escalation = (
            self._repository.get_by_id(
                escalation_id
            )
        )

        if escalation is None:
            return None

        return EscalationDetail(
            escalation_id=(
                escalation.escalation_id
            ),
            escalation_type=(
                escalation.escalation_type
            ),
            severity=(
                escalation.severity
            ),
            provider=(
                escalation.provider
            ),
            capability=(
                escalation.capability
            ),
            message=(
                escalation.message
            ),
            recommendation=(
                escalation.recommendation
            ),
            created_at=(
                escalation.created_at
            ),
            status=(
                escalation.status
            ),
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_history_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation history service.

Provides read-only operational views over escalation
workflow history.

Architecture:

Escalation Workflow Repository
        |
        v
EscalationHistoryService
        |
        +----------------------+
        |                      |
        v                      v
History View             Operations Dashboard
        |
        v
AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- retrieve escalation workflow history;
- filter workflows by status;
- filter workflows by escalation ID;
- provide pending approval workflows;
- provide approved workflows;
- provide rejected workflows;
- provide chronological history.

Does NOT:

- create escalations;
- modify workflow state;
- approve escalations;
- reject escalations;
- send emails;
- change Runtime routing;
- execute provider changes.

This is deliberately a read-only application service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_workflow_repository import (
    EscalationWorkflowRepository,
)

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_workflow_service import (
    EscalationWorkflow,
    EscalationWorkflowStatus,
)


class EscalationHistoryService:
    """
    Read-only application service for escalation history.

    The service provides the Operations Center with a
    stable application contract without exposing the
    repository directly to presentation code.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        repository: EscalationWorkflowRepository,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the history service.
        """

        if repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation workflow repository "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository = repository

    def list_history(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return the complete escalation workflow history.

        Results are supplied by the repository in
        chronological order.
        """

        return self._repository.get_all()

    def get(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow | None:
        """
        Retrieve one escalation workflow.
        """

        if not escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation ID cannot be empty."
            )

        return self._repository.get(
            escalation_id
        )

    def list_by_status(
        self,
        status: EscalationWorkflowStatus,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return workflows matching a specific lifecycle state.
        """

        if status is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Workflow status cannot be None."
            )

        return [
            workflow
            for workflow in self._repository.get_all()
            if workflow.status == status
        ]

    def list_pending_approval(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return escalations currently awaiting human approval.
        """

        return self.list_by_status(
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .AWAITING_APPROVAL
        )

    def list_approved(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return approved escalation workflows.
        """

        return self.list_by_status(
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .APPROVED
        )

    def list_rejected(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return rejected escalation workflows.
        """

        return self.list_by_status(
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .REJECTED
        )

    def list_pending_notification(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return workflows waiting for notification preparation.
        """

        return self.list_by_status(
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .PENDING_NOTIFICATION
        )

    def count_by_status(
        self,
    ) -> dict[
        EscalationWorkflowStatus,
        int,
    ]:
        """
        Return workflow counts grouped by lifecycle status.

        Example:

            {
                AWAITING_APPROVAL: 2,
                APPROVED: 5,
                REJECTED: 1,
                PENDING_NOTIFICATION: 1,
            }

        All statuses are returned, including zero counts.
        """

        counts = {
            status: 0
            for status in EscalationWorkflowStatus
        }

        for workflow in self._repository.get_all():
            counts[
                workflow.status
            ] += 1

        return counts

    def has_pending_approval(
        self,
    ) -> bool:
        """
        Return whether any escalation currently requires
        human approval.
        """

        return bool(
            self.list_pending_approval()
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_notification_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation notification service.

Coordinates operational escalations with the email
notification adapter.

Architecture:

Provider Incident
        |
        v
Escalation Service
        |
        v
EscalationNotificationService
        |
        v
Email Notification Adapter
        |
        v
Human Operations Team


Responsibilities:

- prepare escalation notifications;
- validate notification recipients;
- delegate email preparation to the email adapter;
- keep notification concerns separate from escalation
  detection and incident management.

Does NOT:

- detect provider incidents;
- create provider incidents;
- approve escalations;
- change Runtime routing;
- execute providers;
- send autonomous operational commands.

Human approval remains mandatory.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.email_notification_adapter import (
    EmailNotificationAdapter,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    ProviderEscalation,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EscalationNotificationResult:
    """
    Result of preparing an escalation notification.

    The notification is prepared for delivery by the
    infrastructure adapter.

    Preparation does not itself constitute operational
    approval.
    """

    escalation_id: str

    recipient: str

    subject: str

    body: str


class EscalationNotificationService:
    """
    Application service responsible for preparing
    escalation notifications.

    The service delegates email formatting to the
    existing email adapter and does not contain
    provider-specific notification logic.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        email_adapter: EmailNotificationAdapter,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the notification service.
        """

        if email_adapter is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Email notification adapter cannot be None."
            )

        self._email_adapter = email_adapter

    def prepare_escalation_notification(
        self,
        *,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
        recipient: str,
    ) -> EscalationNotificationResult:
        """
        Prepare an email notification for an escalation.

        No external email delivery occurs here.

        The adapter remains responsible for the concrete
        email representation.
        """

        if escalation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider escalation cannot be None."
            )

        if not recipient.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Notification recipient cannot be empty."
            )

        email = (
            self._email_adapter
            .prepare_escalation_email(
                escalation=escalation,
                recipient=recipient.strip(),
            )
        )

        return EscalationNotificationResult(
            escalation_id=(
                escalation.escalation_id
            ),
            recipient=email.recipient,
            subject=email.subject,
            body=email.body,
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_repository.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
AI Service Provider Manager escalation repository.

Defines the persistence boundary for provider
operational escalations.

This module does not:

- send emails;
- calculate escalation rules;
- execute providers;
- modify Runtime configuration.

Responsibilities:

- store escalations;
- retrieve escalation history;
- provide a persistence abstraction.

Architecture:

EscalationService
        |
        v
EscalationRepository
        |
        v
Storage Implementation

Current implementation:

- in-memory repository.

Future implementations:

- SQLite;
- PostgreSQL;
- enterprise incident platform.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    ProviderEscalation,
)


class EscalationRepository(ABC):
    """
    Persistence contract for provider escalations.

    Application services depend on this abstraction,
    not on a concrete storage implementation.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def save(
        self,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Store one provider escalation.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Escalation save must be implemented."
        )

    @abstractmethod
    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> list[ProviderEscalation]:
        """
        Retrieve all stored escalations.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Escalation get_all must be implemented."
        )

    @abstractmethod
    def get_by_id(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> ProviderEscalation | None:
        """
        Retrieve an escalation by its identifier.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Escalation get_by_id must be implemented."
        )

    @abstractmethod
    def clear(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Clear stored escalations.

        Mainly used by tests.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError(
            "Escalation clear must be implemented."
        )


class InMemoryEscalationRepository(
    EscalationRepository,
):
    """
    In-memory escalation repository.

    Used for:

    - development;
    - testing;
    - hackathon validation.

    It can later be replaced by a persistent
    implementation without changing the service
    contract.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        self._escalations: list[
            ProviderEscalation
        ] = []

    def save(
        self,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Store one escalation.
        """

        if escalation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation cannot be None."
            )

        self._escalations.append(
            escalation
        )

    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> list[ProviderEscalation]:
        """
        Return all stored escalations.

        A copy is returned to prevent callers
        from mutating repository state directly.
        """

        return list(
            self._escalations
        )

    def get_by_id(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> ProviderEscalation | None:
        """
        Retrieve one escalation by ID.
        """

        if not escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation id cannot be empty."
            )

        for escalation in self._escalations:
            if (
                escalation.escalation_id
                == escalation_id
            ):
                return escalation

        return None

    def clear(
        self,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Remove all stored escalations.
        """

        self._escalations.clear()

------------------------------------------------------------
"""
AI Service Provider Manager escalation service.

Creates and manages provider operational escalations.

Architecture:

Provider Intelligence
        |
        v
EscalationService
        |
        v
EscalationRepository


Responsibilities:

- create escalation records;
- validate escalation information;
- persist escalation proposals.


Does NOT:

- send notifications;
- approve changes;
- switch providers;
- execute Runtime actions.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime, timezone
from uuid import uuid4

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_repository import (
    EscalationRepository,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    ProviderEscalation,
    EscalationSeverity,
    EscalationType,
)


class EscalationService:
    """
    Application service for provider escalations.

    Human approval remains mandatory.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        repository: EscalationRepository,
    ) -> None:

        if repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation repository cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository = repository

    def create_escalation(
        self,
        *,
        escalation_type: EscalationType,
        severity: EscalationSeverity,
        provider: str,
        capability: str,
        message: str,
        recommendation: str | None = None,
    ) -> ProviderEscalation:
        """
        Create a provider escalation.

        Example:

        OpenAI latency degradation
        ->
        HIGH escalation
        ->
        Human review required
        """

        if not provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider cannot be empty."
            )

        if not capability.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Capability cannot be empty."
            )

        if not message.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation message cannot be empty."
            )

        escalation = ProviderEscalation(
            escalation_id=str(
                uuid4()
            ),
            escalation_type=(
                escalation_type
            ),
            severity=(
                severity
            ),
            provider=provider,
            capability=capability,
            message=message,
            recommendation=(
                recommendation
            ),
            created_at=(
                datetime.now(
                    timezone.utc
                )
            ),
        )

        self._repository.save(
            escalation
        )

        return escalation

    def list_escalations(
        self,
    ) -> list[ProviderEscalation]:
        """
        Retrieve escalation history.
        """

        return (
            self._repository.get_all()
        )

"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation type service.

Provides aggregated escalation information for the
Escalation Types Overview.

Architecture:

Escalation Repository
        |
        v
EscalationTypeService
        |
        v
EscalationTypeSummary
        |
        v
AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- count escalations by type;
- expose active escalation counts;
- provide dashboard-ready aggregation.

Does NOT:

- create escalations;
- modify escalations;
- approve recommendations;
- send emails;
- change provider routing;
- execute providers.

The repository remains the source of truth.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_repository import (
    EscalationRepository,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    EscalationType,
    EscalationStatus,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EscalationTypeSummary:
    """
    Aggregated information for one escalation type.
    """

    escalation_type: EscalationType

    total_count: int

    active_count: int


class EscalationTypeService:
    """
    Read-only service for escalation type analytics.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        repository: EscalationRepository,
    ) -> None:

        if repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation repository cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository = repository

    def get_summary(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[EscalationTypeSummary, ...]:
        """
        Return escalation counts for every supported type.

        All escalation types are returned, including types
        with zero occurrences, so the Operations Center can
        render a stable UI.
        """

        escalations = (
            self._repository.get_all()
        )

        summaries: list[
            EscalationTypeSummary
        ] = []

        for escalation_type in EscalationType:
            matching = [
                escalation
                for escalation in escalations
                if (
                    escalation.escalation_type
                    == escalation_type
                )
            ]

            active = [
                escalation
                for escalation in matching
                if escalation.status
                in (
                    EscalationStatus.OPEN,
                    EscalationStatus.ACKNOWLEDGED,
                )
            ]

            summaries.append(
                EscalationTypeSummary(
                    escalation_type=(
                        escalation_type
                    ),
                    total_count=len(
                        matching
                    ),
                    active_count=len(
                        active
                    ),
                )
            )

        return tuple(
            summaries
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_workflow_repository.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation workflow repository.

Provides the persistence boundary for human-in-the-loop
escalation workflows.

Architecture:

EscalationWorkflowService
        |
        v
EscalationWorkflowRepository
        |
        v
Persistence Adapter
        |
        v
Escalation History


Responsibilities:

- persist escalation workflow state;
- retrieve workflow state;
- list workflow history.

Does NOT:

- create escalations;
- detect provider incidents;
- send emails;
- approve escalations;
- modify Runtime routing;
- execute provider changes.

The repository stores workflow state only.
Business decisions remain in the application services.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_workflow_service import (
    EscalationWorkflow,
)


class EscalationWorkflowRepository(ABC):
    """
    Repository contract for escalation workflow persistence.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def save(
        self,
        workflow: EscalationWorkflow,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Persist an escalation workflow.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def get(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow | None:
        """
        Retrieve a workflow by escalation ID.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Retrieve all workflow records.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError


class InMemoryEscalationWorkflowRepository(
    EscalationWorkflowRepository,
):
    """
    In-memory workflow repository.

    Used for:

    - Hackathon development;
    - unit testing;
    - integration testing;
    - local demonstrations.

    A SQLite/PostgreSQL adapter can replace this implementation
    later without changing the application service contract.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._workflows: dict[
            str,
            EscalationWorkflow,
        ] = {}

    def save(
        self,
        workflow: EscalationWorkflow,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Save or replace a workflow.
        """

        if workflow is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation workflow cannot be None."
            )

        if not workflow.escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation ID cannot be empty."
            )

        self._workflows[
            workflow.escalation_id
        ] = workflow

    def get(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow | None:
        """
        Retrieve a workflow by escalation ID.
        """

        if not escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation ID cannot be empty."
            )

        return self._workflows.get(
            escalation_id
        )

    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return all persisted workflows.

        Results are ordered by creation time so the
        repository can support the Operations Center
        escalation history view.
        """

        return sorted(
            self._workflows.values(),
            key=lambda workflow: (
                workflow.created_at,
                workflow.escalation_id,
            ),
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\escalation_workflow_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center escalation workflow service.

Coordinates the human-in-the-loop escalation lifecycle.

Architecture:

Provider Incident
        |
        v
Escalation Service
        |
        v
Escalation Workflow Service
        |
        +----------------------+
        |                      |
        v                      v
Notification Service     Approval State
        |                      |
        v                      v
Human Notification      Human Decision
                               |
                    +----------+----------+
                    |                     |
                    v                     v
                 APPROVED              REJECTED


Responsibilities:

- coordinate escalation notification preparation;
- track the human approval state;
- record approval/rejection decisions;
- expose workflow state for the Operations Center;
- preserve the audit trail information required by the
  workflow.

Does NOT:

- execute provider changes;
- modify Runtime routing;
- send autonomous operational commands;
- approve recommendations automatically;
- detect provider incidents;
- perform provider failover.

Human approval is mandatory for operational changes.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from enum import Enum

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_notification_service import (
    EscalationNotificationResult,
    EscalationNotificationService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    ProviderEscalation,
)


class EscalationWorkflowStatus(str, Enum):
    """
    Human-in-the-loop escalation workflow states.
    """

    PENDING_NOTIFICATION = (
        "pending_notification"
    )

    AWAITING_APPROVAL = (
        "awaiting_approval"
    )

    APPROVED = "approved"

    REJECTED = "rejected"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EscalationWorkflow:
    """
    Human-in-the-loop escalation workflow record.
    """

    escalation_id: str

    status: EscalationWorkflowStatus

    created_at: datetime

    notification_recipient: str | None = None

    notification_subject: str | None = None

    approved_at: datetime | None = None

    rejected_at: datetime | None = None

    decision_reason: str | None = None


class EscalationWorkflowService:
    """
    Coordinates the escalation notification and approval
    lifecycle.

    The service deliberately does not execute the action
    represented by an approved recommendation.

    Approval means:

        "A human approved this recommendation."

    It does NOT mean:

        "The system has already changed Runtime routing."
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        notification_service: (
            EscalationNotificationService
        ),
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the escalation workflow service.
        """

        if notification_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation notification service "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._notification_service = (
            notification_service
        )

        self._workflows: dict[
            str,
            EscalationWorkflow,
        ] = {}

    def create_workflow(
        self,
        *,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow:
        """
        Create a workflow awaiting notification.

        No notification is sent at this stage.
        """

        if escalation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider escalation cannot be None."
            )

        if not escalation.escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation ID cannot be empty."
            )

        workflow = EscalationWorkflow(
            escalation_id=(
                escalation.escalation_id
            ),
            status=(
                EscalationWorkflowStatus
                .PENDING_NOTIFICATION
            ),
            created_at=datetime.now(
                timezone.utc
            ),
        )

        self._workflows[
            escalation.escalation_id
        ] = workflow

        return workflow

    def prepare_notification(
        self,
        *,
        escalation: ProviderEscalation,
        recipient: str,
    ) -> EscalationNotificationResult:
        """
        Prepare the human notification and move the
        workflow to awaiting approval.

        Email preparation is delegated to the notification
        service.
        """

        if escalation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider escalation cannot be None."
            )

        workflow = self.get_workflow(
            escalation.escalation_id
        )

        if workflow is None:
            workflow = self.create_workflow(
                escalation=escalation
            )

        if (
            workflow.status
            != EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .PENDING_NOTIFICATION
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation workflow is not awaiting "
                "notification."
            )

        notification = (
            self._notification_service
            .prepare_escalation_notification(
                escalation=escalation,
                recipient=recipient,
            )
        )

        updated = EscalationWorkflow(
            escalation_id=(
                workflow.escalation_id
            ),
            status=(
                EscalationWorkflowStatus
                .AWAITING_APPROVAL
            ),
            created_at=workflow.created_at,
            notification_recipient=(
                notification.recipient
            ),
            notification_subject=(
                notification.subject
            ),
            approved_at=None,
            rejected_at=None,
            decision_reason=None,
        )

        self._workflows[
            escalation.escalation_id
        ] = updated

        return notification

    def approve(
        self,
        *,
        escalation_id: str,
        reason: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow:
        """
        Record a human approval.

        This records approval only.

        It does NOT execute the recommended operational
        change.
        """

        workflow = self._require_workflow(
            escalation_id
        )

        if (
            workflow.status
            != EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .AWAITING_APPROVAL
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation must be awaiting approval "
                "before it can be approved."
            )

        if not reason.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Approval reason cannot be empty."
            )

        approved = EscalationWorkflow(
            escalation_id=(
                workflow.escalation_id
            ),
            status=(
                EscalationWorkflowStatus
                .APPROVED
            ),
            created_at=workflow.created_at,
            notification_recipient=(
                workflow.notification_recipient
            ),
            notification_subject=(
                workflow.notification_subject
            ),
            approved_at=datetime.now(
                timezone.utc
            ),
            rejected_at=None,
            decision_reason=reason.strip(),
        )

        self._workflows[
            escalation_id
        ] = approved

        return approved

    def reject(
        self,
        *,
        escalation_id: str,
        reason: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow:
        """
        Record a human rejection.

        Rejection does not modify provider routing.
        """

        workflow = self._require_workflow(
            escalation_id
        )

        if (
            workflow.status
            != EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .AWAITING_APPROVAL
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation must be awaiting approval "
                "before it can be rejected."
            )

        if not reason.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Rejection reason cannot be empty."
            )

        rejected = EscalationWorkflow(
            escalation_id=(
                workflow.escalation_id
            ),
            status=(
                EscalationWorkflowStatus
                .REJECTED
            ),
            created_at=workflow.created_at,
            notification_recipient=(
                workflow.notification_recipient
            ),
            notification_subject=(
                workflow.notification_subject
            ),
            approved_at=None,
            rejected_at=datetime.now(
                timezone.utc
            ),
            decision_reason=reason.strip(),
        )

        self._workflows[
            escalation_id
        ] = rejected

        return rejected

    def get_workflow(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow | None:
        """
        Retrieve an escalation workflow.
        """

        if not escalation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation ID cannot be empty."
            )

        return self._workflows.get(
            escalation_id
        )

    def list_workflows(
        self,
    ) -> list[EscalationWorkflow]:
        """
        Return all tracked escalation workflows.
        """

        return list(
            self._workflows.values()
        )

    def _require_workflow(
        self,
        escalation_id: str,
    ) -> EscalationWorkflow:
        """
        Retrieve a required workflow.
        """

        workflow = self.get_workflow(
            escalation_id
        )

        if workflow is None:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Escalation workflow not found: "
                f"{escalation_id}"
            )

        return workflow

"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center health dashboard service.

Provides the application-level data contract for the
AI Operations Center health dashboard.

Architecture:

Provider Analytics
        |
        +----------------------+
        |                      |
        v                      v

Provider Health         Escalation Repository
        |                      |
        +----------+-----------+
                   |
                   v
        HealthDashboardService
                   |
                   v
        AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- aggregate provider operational health;
- expose active provider information;
- expose active escalation information;
- provide a dashboard-ready health summary.


Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- change provider routing;
- create escalations;
- send emails;
- render UI;
- make autonomous operational changes.


Important:

"System confidence" represents operational confidence
derived from provider execution success.

It is not an AI quality score.

Quality intelligence can be incorporated later when
the approved quality evaluation framework is connected.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_repository import (
    EscalationRepository,
)

from app.application.analytics.ai_platform.provider_analytics_service import (
    ProviderAnalyticsService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    EscalationSeverity,
    EscalationStatus,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AIHealthDashboard:
    """
    Dashboard-ready AI Operations Center health data.
    """

    system_confidence: float

    active_providers: tuple[str, ...]

    monitoring_active: bool

    active_escalations: int

    high_priority_escalations: int


class AIHealthDashboardService:
    """
    Generates the AI Operations Center health summary.

    This service is deliberately read-only.

    It aggregates existing analytics and escalation
    information instead of maintaining another source
    of operational truth.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        analytics_service: ProviderAnalyticsService,
        escalation_repository: EscalationRepository,
    ) -> None:

        if analytics_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "ProviderAnalyticsService cannot be None."
            )

        if escalation_repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "EscalationRepository cannot be None."
            )

        self._analytics_service = (
            analytics_service
        )

        self._escalation_repository = (
            escalation_repository
        )

    def generate_dashboard(
        self,
    ) -> AIHealthDashboard:
        """
        Generate the current AI Operations health view.
        """

        analytics = (
            self._analytics_service
            .generate_summary()
        )

        escalations = (
            self._escalation_repository
            .get_all()
        )

        active_escalations = [
            escalation
            for escalation in escalations
            if escalation.status
            in (
                EscalationStatus.OPEN,
                EscalationStatus.ACKNOWLEDGED,
            )
        ]

        high_priority_escalations = [
            escalation
            for escalation in active_escalations
            if escalation.severity
            in (
                EscalationSeverity.HIGH,
                EscalationSeverity.CRITICAL,
            )
        ]

        return AIHealthDashboard(
            system_confidence=(
                analytics.success_rate
            ),
            active_providers=(
                analytics.providers
            ),
            monitoring_active=True,
            active_escalations=(
                len(active_escalations)
            ),
            high_priority_escalations=(
                len(high_priority_escalations)
            ),
        )

This module does not:

Provider Management Models

"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center operational event contract.

Defines the immutable operational facts that can be emitted by
the Runtime, provider-management services, and future agent
framework integrations.

Architecture:

Agent / Runtime / Provider Service
                |
                v
        OperationalEvent
                |
                v
     Operational Event Service
                |
                v
       Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- define a stable operational event contract;
- define supported event categories;
- define operational severity;
- carry correlation and structured metadata;
- remain independent from HTTP, persistence, providers,
  agents, and Runtime implementation details.

Does NOT:

- execute agents;
- execute providers;
- change Runtime routing;
- create autonomous actions;
- persist events;
- send notifications;
- approve escalations.

Operational events represent FACTS, not COMMANDS.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum
from types import MappingProxyType
from typing import Any, Mapping
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


class OperationalEventType(str, Enum):
    """
    Supported categories of operational facts.
    """

    PROVIDER_HEALTH_CHANGED = (
        "provider_health_changed"
    )

    PROVIDER_INCIDENT_CREATED = (
        "provider_incident_created"
    )

    PROVIDER_RECOVERED = (
        "provider_recovered"
    )

    ESCALATION_CREATED = (
        "escalation_created"
    )

    ESCALATION_APPROVAL_REQUIRED = (
        "escalation_approval_required"
    )

    AGENT_EXECUTION_FAILED = (
        "agent_execution_failed"
    )

    AGENT_EXECUTION_COMPLETED = (
        "agent_execution_completed"
    )


class OperationalEventSeverity(str, Enum):
    """
    Operational severity associated with an event.
    """

    INFO = "info"

    WARNING = "warning"

    ERROR = "error"

    CRITICAL = "critical"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OperationalEvent:
    """
    Immutable operational fact.

    Events are deliberately generic so that both the future
    internal AI Operations/Quality framework and the external
    AI Agents framework can emit the same operational contract
    without being coupled to the Operations Center implementation.

    Metadata is structured context only. It must not contain
    executable commands or instructions.
    """

    event_type: OperationalEventType

    severity: OperationalEventSeverity

    source: str

    component: str

    occurred_at: datetime

    message: str

    event_id: UUID

    correlation_id: str | None

    metadata: Mapping[str, Any]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        *,
        event_type: OperationalEventType,
        severity: OperationalEventSeverity,
        source: str,
        component: str,
        occurred_at: datetime,
        message: str,
        correlation_id: str | None = None,
        metadata: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
    ) -> "OperationalEvent":
        """
        Create a validated operational event.

        A new UUID is generated for every event.

        The event timestamp must be timezone-aware so that
        operational events remain unambiguous across environments.
        """

        if not source.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event source "
                "cannot be empty."
            )

        if not component.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event component "
                "cannot be empty."
            )

        if not message.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event message "
                "cannot be empty."
            )

        if occurred_at.tzinfo is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event timestamp "
                "must be timezone-aware."
            )

        if (
            correlation_id is not None
            and not correlation_id.strip()
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event correlation ID "
                "cannot be empty."
            )

        event_metadata = (
            {}
            if metadata is None
            else dict(metadata)
        )

        return cls(
            event_type=event_type,
            severity=severity,
            source=source.strip(),
            component=component.strip(),
            occurred_at=occurred_at,
            message=message.strip(),
            event_id=uuid4(),
            correlation_id=(
                correlation_id.strip()
                if correlation_id is not None
                else None
            ),
            metadata=MappingProxyType(
                event_metadata
            ),
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\operational_event_repository.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center operational event repository.

Provides the persistence boundary for immutable operational
events.

Architecture:

OperationalEvent
        |
        v
OperationalEventRepository
        |
        v
In-Memory / Future Persistent Store


Responsibilities:

- define the repository contract;
- store operational events;
- retrieve events deterministically;
- keep persistence concerns outside the event model.

Does NOT:

- interpret event meaning;
- execute agents;
- execute providers;
- change Runtime routing;
- create autonomous actions;
- send notifications.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod

from app.application.provider_management.operational_event import (
    OperationalEvent,
)


class OperationalEventRepository(ABC):
    """
    Persistence boundary for operational events.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def save(
        self,
        event: OperationalEvent,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Persist one operational event.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def get_by_id(
        self,
        event_id,
    ) -> OperationalEvent | None:
        """
        Return an event by its unique identifier.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[
        OperationalEvent,
        ...
    ]:
        """
        Return all stored events in deterministic order.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError

    @abstractmethod
    def clear(self) -> None:
        """
        Clear repository state.

        Intended primarily for isolated tests and demonstrations.
        """

        raise NotImplementedError


class InMemoryOperationalEventRepository(
    OperationalEventRepository
):
    """
    In-memory operational event repository.

    This implementation is intentionally small and deterministic.
    It can later be replaced by a persistent implementation
    without changing the application service contract.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._events: dict[
            object,
            OperationalEvent,
        ] = {}

    def save(
        self,
        event: OperationalEvent,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Store an operational event.
        """

        if event is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event cannot be None."
            )

        self._events[
            event.event_id
        ] = event

    def get_by_id(
        self,
        event_id,
    ) -> OperationalEvent | None:
        """
        Retrieve an event by ID.
        """

        if event_id is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event ID cannot be None."
            )

        return self._events.get(
            event_id
        )

    def get_all(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[
        OperationalEvent,
        ...
    ]:
        """
        Return events ordered by occurrence time and event ID.
        """

        return tuple(
            sorted(
                self._events.values(),
                key=lambda event: (
                    event.occurred_at,
                    str(event.event_id),
                ),
            )
        )

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """
        Remove all stored events.
        """

        self._events.clear()

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\operational_event_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center operational event service.

Provides the application boundary for recording operational
facts emitted by Runtime components, provider services, and
future agent frameworks.

Architecture:

Agent / Runtime / Provider Service
                |
                v
       OperationalEventService
                |
                v
       OperationalEventRepository
                |
                v
        Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- accept validated operational events;
- persist events through the repository abstraction;
- retrieve operational events;
- provide a stable application boundary for future
  internal and external agent integrations.

Does NOT:

- execute agents;
- execute providers;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve escalations;
- perform autonomous remediation;
- send notifications;
- access persistence directly.

Operational events remain immutable facts.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from uuid import UUID

from app.application.provider_management.operational_event import (
    OperationalEvent,
)

from app.application.provider_management.operational_event_repository import (
    OperationalEventRepository,
)


class OperationalEventService:
    """
    Application service for operational event management.

    The service deliberately delegates persistence to the
    repository abstraction and contains no infrastructure logic.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        repository: OperationalEventRepository,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the operational event service.
        """

        if repository is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event repository "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository = repository

    def record(
        self,
        event: OperationalEvent,
    ) -> OperationalEvent:
        """
        Record one operational event.

        The event itself is immutable. The service therefore
        returns the same event after successful persistence.
        """

        if event is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event cannot be None."
            )

        self._repository.save(event)

        return event

    def get_event(
        self,
        event_id: UUID,
    ) -> OperationalEvent | None:
        """
        Retrieve one operational event by ID.
        """

        if event_id is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event ID cannot be None."
            )

        return self._repository.get_by_id(
            event_id
        )

    def list_events(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[
        OperationalEvent,
        ...
    ]:
        """
        Return all operational events in repository order.
        """

        return self._repository.get_all()

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """
        Clear operational event state.

        Intended for isolated tests and demonstrations.
        """

        self._repository.clear()

------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center dashboard API.

Provides the application-facing read-only boundary for
the Operations Center dashboard.

Architecture:

Operations Center Dashboard Service
                |
                v
      OperationsCenterDashboardFacade
                |
                v
        OperationsCenterDashboardAPI
                |
                v
          HTTP Router

Responsibilities:

- expose the dashboard application contract;
- delegate dashboard generation to the facade;
- keep HTTP concerns outside the application layer;
- preserve compatibility with explicit provider input;
- provide a stable boundary for future Operations Center
  integrations.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- execute agents;
- modify Runtime routing;
- access repositories directly;
- approve escalations;
- send notifications;
- perform autonomous remediation.

The dashboard is read-only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_facade import (
    OperationsCenterDashboardFacade,
)

from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_service import (
    ActiveProvider,
)


_UNSET = object()


class OperationsCenterDashboardAPI:
    """
    Application-facing API for the Operations Center dashboard.

    The API deliberately contains no business logic.
    It delegates dashboard generation to the facade.

    For compatibility and deterministic testing, callers may
    explicitly supply active providers.

    Calling get_dashboard() without provider input preserves
    the facade's normal provider-resolution behaviour.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        facade: OperationsCenterDashboardFacade,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the dashboard API.
        """

        if facade is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operations Center dashboard facade "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._facade = facade

    def get_dashboard(
        self,
        *,
        active_providers: (
            tuple[
                ActiveProvider,
                ...
            ]
            | None
            | object
        ) = _UNSET,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Return the current Operations Center dashboard.

        Resolution rules:

        - omitted:
            delegate to the facade without explicit provider input;

        - explicit tuple:
            delegate using the supplied providers;

        - explicit None:
            reject invalid provider input.

        The API performs no dashboard business logic.
        """

        if active_providers is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Active providers cannot be None."
            )

        if active_providers is _UNSET:
            return self._facade.get_dashboard()

        return self._facade.get_dashboard(
            active_providers=active_providers,
        )

"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center dashboard composition.

Composition root for the read-only Operations Center
dashboard, provider-health application stack, and
operational-event read API.

This module owns dependency construction for the
Operations Center application boundary.

It does not execute providers, agents, Runtime routing,
or autonomous remediation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_history_service import (
    EscalationHistoryService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.escalation_workflow_repository import (
    InMemoryEscalationWorkflowRepository,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_api import (
    OperationsCenterDashboardAPI,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_facade import (
    OperationsCenterDashboardFacade,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_service import (
    OperationsCenterDashboardService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_operational_events_api import (
    OperationsCenterOperationalEventsAPI,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_provider_service import (
    OperationsCenterProviderService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operational_event_repository import (
    InMemoryOperationalEventRepository,
)
from app.application.provider_management.operational_event_service import (
    OperationalEventService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_application_service import (
    ProviderHealthApplicationService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_monitor_service import (
    ProviderHealthMonitorService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.provider_incident_service import (
    InMemoryProviderIncidentRepository,
    ProviderIncidentService,
)
from app.application.provider_management.provider_operational_event_service import (
    ProviderOperationalEventService,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OperationsCenterComposition:
    """
    Composed Operations Center application graph.

    Exposes the application-facing boundaries needed for
    controlled integration testing and demonstration.
    """

    dashboard_api: OperationsCenterDashboardAPI

    health_application_service: (
        ProviderHealthApplicationService
    )

    operational_event_service: (
        OperationalEventService
    )

    operational_events_api: (
        OperationsCenterOperationalEventsAPI
    )


def create_operations_center_composition() -> (
    OperationsCenterComposition
):
    """
    Construct the complete Operations Center application graph.

    A single ProviderHealthMonitorService is shared between
    health ingestion and provider intelligence.

    A single OperationalEventService is shared by provider
    operational-event adapters and the read-only operational
    events API.

    Provider incidents therefore emit operational facts into
    the same event store exposed by the Operations Center API.
    """

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Provider health state
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    health_monitor = (
        ProviderHealthMonitorService()
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Operational event persistence
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    operational_event_repository = (
        InMemoryOperationalEventRepository()
    )

    operational_event_service = (
        OperationalEventService(
            repository=(
                operational_event_repository
            )
        )
    )

    provider_operational_event_service = (
        ProviderOperationalEventService(
            operational_event_service=(
                operational_event_service
            )
        )
    )

    operational_events_api = (
        OperationsCenterOperationalEventsAPI(
            operational_event_service=(
                operational_event_service
            )
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Provider incidents
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    incident_repository = (
        InMemoryProviderIncidentRepository()
    )

    incident_service = (
        ProviderIncidentService(
            repository=incident_repository,
            operational_event_service=(
                provider_operational_event_service
            ),
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Health application boundary
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    health_application_service = (
        ProviderHealthApplicationService(
            health_monitor=health_monitor,
            incident_service=incident_service,
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Escalation history
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    workflow_repository = (
        InMemoryEscalationWorkflowRepository()
    )

    history_service = (
        EscalationHistoryService(
            repository=workflow_repository
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Provider intelligence
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    provider_service = (
        OperationsCenterProviderService(
            health_monitor=health_monitor
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Dashboard application service
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    dashboard_service = (
        OperationsCenterDashboardService(
            history_service=history_service,
            provider_service=provider_service,
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Presentation facade
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    facade = (
        OperationsCenterDashboardFacade(
            dashboard_service=dashboard_service
        )
    )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------
    # Application API
    # ---------------------------------------------------------

    dashboard_api = (
        OperationsCenterDashboardAPI(
            facade=facade
        )
    )

    return OperationsCenterComposition(
        dashboard_api=dashboard_api,
        health_application_service=(
            health_application_service
        ),
        operational_event_service=(
            operational_event_service
        ),
        operational_events_api=(
            operational_events_api
        ),
    )


def create_operations_center_dashboard_api() -> (
    OperationsCenterDashboardAPI
):
    """
    Backward-compatible dashboard API composition entry point.

    Existing callers that only require the dashboard continue
    to receive the dashboard API.

    The complete application graph is composed through
    create_operations_center_composition().
    """

    composition = (
        create_operations_center_composition()
    )

    return composition.dashboard_api
