FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\operations_center_dashboard_facade.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center dashboard facade.

Provides a stable application-facing contract for the
Operations Center presentation/API layer.

Architecture:

Provider Health
      |
Escalations
      |
Workflow
      |
History Service
      |
Dashboard Service
      |
      v
OperationsCenterDashboardFacade
      |
      v
API / Frontend


Responsibilities:

- expose dashboard information to presentation layers;
- convert domain/application read models into
  presentation-safe dictionaries;
- keep frontend/API code independent from internal
  workflow objects;
- provide stable field names for the Operations Center UI;
- preserve compatibility with explicit provider input.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve escalations;
- reject escalations;
- send emails;
- modify persistence;
- perform autonomous operational actions.

This is a read-only presentation boundary.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_service import (
    ActiveProvider,
    OperationsCenterDashboard,
    OperationsCenterDashboardService,
)


_UNSET = object()


class OperationsCenterDashboardFacade:
    """
    Presentation-facing facade for the AI Operations Center.

    Provider resolution normally remains below this boundary.

    For compatibility and deterministic testing, callers may
    explicitly supply active providers.

    Calling get_dashboard() without provider input delegates
    provider resolution to OperationsCenterDashboardService.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        dashboard_service: OperationsCenterDashboardService,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the dashboard facade.
        """

        if dashboard_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operations Center dashboard service "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._dashboard_service = (
            dashboard_service
        )

    def get_dashboard(
        self,
        *,
        active_providers: (
            tuple[
                ActiveProvider,
                ...
            ]
            | None
            | object
        ) = _UNSET,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Return a frontend-safe dashboard representation.

        Resolution rules:

        - omitted:
            use provider resolution configured in the
            dashboard service;

        - explicit tuple:
            use the supplied providers;

        - explicit None:
            reject the invalid input.

        This preserves the existing application contract
        while supporting the new production composition.
        """

        if active_providers is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Active providers cannot be None."
            )

        if active_providers is _UNSET:
            dashboard = (
                self._dashboard_service
                .build_dashboard()
            )
        else:
            dashboard = (
                self._dashboard_service
                .build_dashboard(
                    active_providers=(
                        active_providers
                    )
                )
            )

        return self._serialize_dashboard(
            dashboard
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _serialize_dashboard(
        dashboard: OperationsCenterDashboard,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Convert the dashboard read model into a stable
        presentation contract.
        """

        return {
            "system_confidence": (
                dashboard.system_confidence
            ),
            "monitoring_status": (
                dashboard.monitoring_status.value
            ),
            "active_providers": [
                OperationsCenterDashboardFacade
                ._serialize_provider(provider)
                for provider
                in dashboard.active_providers
            ],
            "active_escalations": (
                dashboard.active_escalations
            ),
            "pending_approvals": (
                dashboard.pending_approvals
            ),
            "approved_escalations": (
                dashboard.approved_escalations
            ),
            "rejected_escalations": (
                dashboard.rejected_escalations
            ),
            "pending_notifications": (
                dashboard.pending_notifications
            ),
            "total_escalations": (
                dashboard.total_escalations
            ),
        }

    @staticmethod
    def _serialize_provider(
        provider: ActiveProvider,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Convert an active provider into a frontend-safe
        representation.
        """

        return {
            "provider": provider.provider,
            "model": provider.model,
            "capabilities": list(
                provider.capabilities
            ),
            "active": provider.active,
        }

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\operations_center_dashboard_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center dashboard service.

Provides a read-only, user-friendly operational view
for the AI Operations Center.

Architecture:

Provider Intelligence
        |
        v
Provider Health / Escalations / Workflow
        |
        v
OperationsCenterDashboardService
        |
        v
Dashboard Read Model
        |
        v
Frontend / API


Responsibilities:

- aggregate provider operational status;
- expose active providers;
- expose monitoring status;
- expose escalation counts;
- expose pending human approvals;
- calculate an operational confidence score;
- provide a stable presentation-independent read model.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve recommendations;
- send emails;
- modify escalation state;
- modify provider configuration.

This is a read-only application service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_history_service import (
    EscalationHistoryService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_workflow_service import (
    EscalationWorkflowStatus,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_provider_service import (
        OperationsCenterProviderService,
    )


class MonitoringStatus(str, Enum):
    """
    Overall monitoring state shown by the Operations Center.
    """

    HEALTHY = "healthy"

    ATTENTION_REQUIRED = (
        "attention_required"
    )

    INCIDENT = "incident"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ActiveProvider:
    """
    Provider information displayed by the Operations Center.
    """

    provider: str

    model: str

    capabilities: tuple[str, ...]

    active: bool = True


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OperationsCenterDashboard:
    """
    User-friendly Operations Center dashboard read model.
    """

    system_confidence: float

    monitoring_status: MonitoringStatus

    active_providers: tuple[
        ActiveProvider,
        ...
    ]

    active_escalations: int

    pending_approvals: int

    approved_escalations: int

    rejected_escalations: int

    pending_notifications: int

    total_escalations: int


# Sentinel used to distinguish:
#
#     build_dashboard()
#
# from:
#
#     build_dashboard(active_providers=None)
#
# The former means "obtain providers from the configured
# provider service".
#
# The latter is invalid input and must remain rejected.
_UNSET = object()


class OperationsCenterDashboardService:
    """
    Builds the read-only Operations Center dashboard.

    Provider information can be explicitly supplied for
    deterministic tests and controlled application use.

    When active providers are omitted entirely, the configured
    OperationsCenterProviderService is used.

    Explicit None remains invalid.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        history_service: EscalationHistoryService,
        provider_service: (
            OperationsCenterProviderService
            | None
        ) = None,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the dashboard service.
        """

        if history_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation history service "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._history_service = (
            history_service
        )

        self._provider_service = (
            provider_service
        )

    def build_dashboard(
        self,
        *,
        active_providers: (
            tuple[
                ActiveProvider,
                ...
            ]
            | None
            | object
        ) = _UNSET,
    ) -> OperationsCenterDashboard:
        """
        Build the current Operations Center dashboard.

        Provider resolution rules:

        1. Explicit tuple:
               use the supplied providers.

        2. Explicit None:
               reject the invalid input.

        3. Argument omitted:
               use the configured provider service.

        This distinction preserves the original dashboard
        contract while allowing the production composition
        root to obtain real provider intelligence.
        """

        if active_providers is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Active providers cannot be None."
            )

        if active_providers is _UNSET:
            if self._provider_service is None:
                raise ValueError(
                    "Active providers were not supplied "
                    "and no provider service is configured."
                )

            resolved_active_providers = (
                self._provider_service
                .get_active_providers()
            )
        else:
            resolved_active_providers = (
                active_providers
            )

        counts = (
            self._history_service
            .count_by_status()
        )

        pending_approvals = counts[
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .AWAITING_APPROVAL
        ]

        approved = counts[
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .APPROVED
        ]

        rejected = counts[
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .REJECTED
        ]

        pending_notifications = counts[
            EscalationWorkflowStatus
            .PENDING_NOTIFICATION
        ]

        total_escalations = sum(
            counts.values()
        )

        active_escalations = (
            pending_approvals
            + pending_notifications
        )

        monitoring_status = (
            self._determine_monitoring_status(
                active_escalations=(
                    active_escalations
                ),
                pending_approvals=(
                    pending_approvals
                ),
            )
        )

        confidence = (
            self._calculate_confidence(
                active_escalations=(
                    active_escalations
                ),
                pending_approvals=(
                    pending_approvals
                ),
                total_escalations=(
                    total_escalations
                ),
            )
        )

        return OperationsCenterDashboard(
            system_confidence=confidence,
            monitoring_status=monitoring_status,
            active_providers=(
                resolved_active_providers
            ),
            active_escalations=(
                active_escalations
            ),
            pending_approvals=(
                pending_approvals
            ),
            approved_escalations=approved,
            rejected_escalations=rejected,
            pending_notifications=(
                pending_notifications
            ),
            total_escalations=(
                total_escalations
            ),
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _determine_monitoring_status(
        *,
        active_escalations: int,
        pending_approvals: int,
    ) -> MonitoringStatus:
        """
        Determine the dashboard monitoring status.

        Critical operational state is represented by the
        existence of active escalations.

        Pending human approval is explicitly surfaced.
        """

        if active_escalations > 0:
            return MonitoringStatus.INCIDENT

        if pending_approvals > 0:
            return (
                MonitoringStatus
                .ATTENTION_REQUIRED
            )

        return MonitoringStatus.HEALTHY

    @staticmethod
    def _calculate_confidence(
        *,
        active_escalations: int,
        pending_approvals: int,
        total_escalations: int,
    ) -> float:
        """
        Calculate a deterministic dashboard confidence score.

        This is deliberately a simple operational indicator
        for the Hackathon.

        It is NOT an AI model confidence score.

        Rules:

        - 100 when there are no escalations;
        - subtract 15 per active escalation;
        - pending approval receives no additional penalty;
        - never below 0.

        Pending approvals are already represented in
        active_escalations, so applying another penalty would
        double-count the same operational condition.
        """

        if total_escalations == 0:
            return 100.0

        score = 100.0

        score -= (
            active_escalations * 15.0
        )

        return max(
            0.0,
            min(100.0, score),
        )

"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center operational events API.

Provides the application-facing read-only boundary for
operational event history.

Architecture:

OperationalEventRepository
        |
        v
OperationalEventService
        |
        v
OperationsCenterOperationalEventsAPI
        |
        v
HTTP Router / Operations Center UI

Responsibilities:

- expose operational event history;
- delegate retrieval to OperationalEventService;
- convert OperationalEvent objects into stable dictionaries;
- keep presentation/API consumers independent from
  internal event dataclasses and enums.

Does NOT:

- create events;
- modify events;
- execute providers;
- execute agents;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve escalations;
- perform autonomous actions.

Operational events remain immutable facts.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

from app.application.provider_management.operational_event_service import (
    OperationalEventService,
)


class OperationsCenterOperationalEventsAPI:
    """
    Read-only application API for operational event history.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        operational_event_service: OperationalEventService,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the operational events API.
        """

        if operational_event_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Operational event service "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._operational_event_service = (
            operational_event_service
        )

    def list_events(
        self,
    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        """
        Return all operational events in a
        presentation-safe representation.
        """

        events = (
            self._operational_event_service
            .list_events()
        )

        return [
            self._serialize_event(event)
            for event in events
        ]

    @staticmethod
    def _serialize_event(
        event: Any,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Convert an OperationalEvent into a stable
        presentation contract.
        """

        return {
            "event_id": str(
                event.event_id
            ),
            "event_type": (
                event.event_type.value
            ),
            "severity": (
                event.severity.value
            ),
            "source": event.source,
            "component": event.component,
            "occurred_at": (
                event.occurred_at.isoformat()
            ),
            "message": event.message,
            "correlation_id": (
                event.correlation_id
            ),
            "metadata": dict(
                event.metadata
            ),
        }

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\operations_center_provider_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center provider service.

Bridges provider health intelligence into the Operations
Center dashboard provider read model.

Architecture:

Provider Health Monitor
        |
        v
ProviderHealthReadModel
        |
        v
OperationsCenterProviderService
        |
        v
ActiveProvider
        |
        v
OperationsCenterDashboardService


Responsibilities:

- obtain provider health read models;
- group capabilities by provider/model;
- expose dashboard-safe active provider information;
- preserve provider health status separately from provider
  identity;
- keep provider health calculation outside the dashboard.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- perform health probes;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve recommendations;
- perform failover;
- send notifications;
- modify provider configuration.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from app.application.provider_management.operations_center_dashboard_service import (
    ActiveProvider,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_monitor_service import (
    ProviderHealthMonitorService,
)


class OperationsCenterProviderService:
    """
    Provides provider information for the Operations Center.

    Provider health remains owned by ProviderHealthMonitorService.
    This service is only an application-level adapter for the
    dashboard.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        health_monitor: ProviderHealthMonitorService,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the provider service.
        """

        if health_monitor is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider health monitor cannot be None."
            )

        self._health_monitor = health_monitor

    def get_active_providers(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[
        ActiveProvider,
        ...
    ]:
        """
        Return providers currently known to the health monitor.

        A provider remains visible to the Operations Center even
        when degraded or unavailable.

        This is intentional: an unavailable provider must not
        disappear from the dashboard because that would hide
        the incident.
        """

        health_records = (
            self._health_monitor
            .get_all_provider_health()
        )

        providers: list[
            ActiveProvider
        ] = []

        for record in health_records:
            if record.model is None:
                continue

            capability = record.capability

            capabilities: tuple[str, ...]

            if capability is None:
                capabilities = ()
            else:
                capabilities = (
                    capability,
                )

            providers.append(
                ActiveProvider(
                    provider=record.provider,
                    model=record.model,
                    capabilities=capabilities,
                    active=record.active,
                )
            )

        return tuple(providers)

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_comparison_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center provider/model comparison service.

Provides comparative analytics across providers and models.

Architecture:

Provider Usage Analytics
        |
        v
Provider Comparison Service
        |
        v
ProviderComparison
        |
        v
Capability Optimisation
        |
        v
Human Review


Responsibilities:

- compare provider/model performance;
- expose comparable operational metrics;
- group comparisons by capability;
- provide decision-support information.


Does NOT:

- change provider routing;
- execute providers;
- approve recommendations;
- send notifications;
- modify Runtime configuration.

Comparison analytics are advisory intelligence only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from decimal import Decimal

from src.ai_runtime.providers.usage.models import (
    ProviderUsageEvent,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderComparison:
    """
    Comparative operational metrics for one
    provider/model combination.
    """

    provider: str

    model: str

    capability: str

    total_requests: int

    successful_requests: int

    success_rate: float

    total_input_tokens: int

    total_output_tokens: int

    total_tokens: int

    average_latency_ms: float

    total_cost: Decimal

    average_cost_per_request: Decimal


class ProviderComparisonService:
    """
    Generates provider/model comparison analytics.

    The service operates on supplied usage events and
    does not own persistence.
    """

    def compare(
        self,
        *,
        events: list[ProviderUsageEvent],
    ) -> tuple[ProviderComparison, ...]:
        """
        Compare provider/model combinations.

        Results are grouped by:

            provider + model + capability

        This prevents unrelated capabilities from being
        combined into one misleading performance metric.
        """

        if events is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Usage events cannot be None."
            )

        grouped: dict[
            tuple[str, str, str],
            list[ProviderUsageEvent],
        ] = {}

        for event in events:
            key = (
                event.provider,
                event.model,
                event.capability,
            )

            grouped.setdefault(
                key,
                [],
            ).append(event)

        comparisons: list[
            ProviderComparison
        ] = []

        for (
            provider,
            model,
            capability,
        ), provider_events in sorted(
            grouped.items()
        ):
            total_requests = len(
                provider_events
            )

            successful_requests = sum(
                1
                for event in provider_events
                if event.success
            )

            total_input_tokens = sum(
                event.input_tokens
                for event in provider_events
            )

            total_output_tokens = sum(
                event.output_tokens
                for event in provider_events
            )

            total_tokens = (
                total_input_tokens
                + total_output_tokens
            )

            total_latency = sum(
                event.latency_ms
                for event in provider_events
            )

            average_latency_ms = (
                total_latency
                / total_requests
            )

            total_cost = sum(
                (
                    event.estimated_cost
                    for event in provider_events
                ),
                Decimal("0"),
            )

            average_cost_per_request = (
                total_cost
                / Decimal(total_requests)
            )

            success_rate = (
                successful_requests
                / total_requests
            ) * 100.0

            comparisons.append(
                ProviderComparison(
                    provider=provider,
                    model=model,
                    capability=capability,
                    total_requests=(
                        total_requests
                    ),
                    successful_requests=(
                        successful_requests
                    ),
                    success_rate=(
                        success_rate
                    ),
                    total_input_tokens=(
                        total_input_tokens
                    ),
                    total_output_tokens=(
                        total_output_tokens
                    ),
                    total_tokens=(
                        total_tokens
                    ),
                    average_latency_ms=(
                        average_latency_ms
                    ),
                    total_cost=total_cost,
                    average_cost_per_request=(
                        average_cost_per_request
                    ),
                )
            )

        return tuple(comparisons)

    def compare_capability(
        self,
        *,
        capability: str,
        events: list[ProviderUsageEvent],
    ) -> tuple[ProviderComparison, ...]:
        """
        Compare providers for one specific capability.
        """

        if not capability.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Capability cannot be empty."
            )

        filtered_events = [
            event
            for event in events
            if event.capability
            == capability
        ]

        return self.compare(
            events=filtered_events
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_health_application_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot provider health application service.

Provides the controlled application boundary for ingesting
provider health observations.

Architecture:

Provider Health Probe / Adapter
        |
        v
ProviderHealthApplicationService
        |
        +-----------------------------+
        |                             |
        v                             v
ProviderHealthMonitorService   ProviderIncidentService
        |                             |
        v                             v
Health State / Read Model       Incident Lifecycle
        |                             |
        +-------------+---------------+
                      |
                      v
               Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- accept explicit provider health observations;
- delegate health processing to the existing monitor;
- preserve ProviderHealthService as the authority for
  health-state calculation;
- process operational incident transitions;
- return a stable application result;
- keep provider probes and HTTP concerns outside this service.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- perform health probes;
- change Runtime routing;
- select alternative providers;
- send notifications directly;
- approve operational actions;
- modify infrastructure;
- perform autonomous remediation.

This is an application orchestration service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_models import (
    ProviderHealthObservation,
    ProviderHealthStatus,
    ProviderHealthTransition,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_monitor_service import (
    ProviderHealthMonitorService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_incident_service import (
    ProviderIncident,
    ProviderIncidentService,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderHealthApplicationResult:
    """
    Stable result returned after processing a provider health
    observation.
    """

    provider: str

    model: str

    capability: str

    status: ProviderHealthStatus

    transition: ProviderHealthTransition

    incident: ProviderIncident | None


class ProviderHealthApplicationService:
    """
    Application boundary for provider health ingestion.

    The service coordinates the existing provider health
    monitor and provider incident service.

    It deliberately does not create a second
    ProviderHealthService. The monitor remains the single
    owner of provider health state.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        health_monitor: ProviderHealthMonitorService,
        incident_service: ProviderIncidentService,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the provider health application service.
        """

        if health_monitor is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider health monitor "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        if incident_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider incident service "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        self._health_monitor = (
            health_monitor
        )

        self._incident_service = (
            incident_service
        )

    def observe(
        self,
        observation: ProviderHealthObservation,
    ) -> ProviderHealthApplicationResult:
        """
        Process one provider health observation.

        Health calculation and provider read-model state remain
        owned by ProviderHealthMonitorService.

        Incident lifecycle remains owned by
        ProviderIncidentService.
        """

        if observation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider health observation "
                "cannot be None."
            )

        transition = (
            self._health_monitor
            .observe(observation)
        )

        incident = (
            self._incident_service
            .process_transition(transition)
        )

        return ProviderHealthApplicationResult(
            provider=observation.provider,
            model=observation.model,
            capability=observation.capability,
            status=observation.status,
            transition=transition.transition,
            incident=incident,
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_health_escalation_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center provider health escalation service.

Bridges provider availability incidents into the existing
EscalationService.

Architecture:

Provider Health Service
        |
        v
Provider Incident Service
        |
        v
ProviderHealthEscalationService
        |
        v
EscalationService
        |
        v
EscalationRepository
        |
        v
AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- identify provider outage incidents that require escalation;
- translate provider incidents into operational escalations;
- determine escalation severity;
- preserve provider/capability information;
- create escalations through the existing EscalationService.

Does NOT:

- send emails;
- approve escalations;
- change Runtime routing;
- execute providers;
- perform autonomous failover;
- directly persist escalation records.

Human approval remains mandatory.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from app.application.provider_management.escalation_service import (
    EscalationService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.models import (
    EscalationSeverity,
    EscalationType,
    ProviderEscalation,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_models import (
    ProviderHealthStatus,
    ProviderIncident,
)


class ProviderHealthEscalationService:
    """
    Converts sustained provider outages into operational
    escalation records.

    The service deliberately sits between incident
    detection and notification.

    Example:

        OpenAI unavailable
                |
                v
        ProviderIncident
                |
                v
        ProviderHealthEscalationService
                |
                v
        HIGH / CRITICAL escalation
                |
                v
        Human review
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        escalation_service: EscalationService,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the health escalation service.
        """

        if escalation_service is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Escalation service cannot be None."
            )

        self._escalation_service = (
            escalation_service
        )

    def escalate_incident(
        self,
        *,
        incident: ProviderIncident,
    ) -> ProviderEscalation | None:
        """
        Create an escalation for a provider outage.

        Only unavailable provider incidents are escalated.

        Already recovered incidents are not escalated as
        active outages.

        The escalation remains advisory and requires human
        review before any operational change.
        """

        if incident is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider incident cannot be None."
            )

        if not incident.provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider cannot be empty."
            )

        if (
            incident.status
            != ProviderHealthStatus.UNAVAILABLE
        ):
            return None

        severity = self._determine_severity(
            incident
        )

        capability = (
            self._format_capability_scope(
                incident
            )
        )

        message = (
            self._build_message(
                incident
            )
        )

        recommendation = (
            self._build_recommendation(
                incident
            )
        )

        return (
            self._escalation_service
            .create_escalation(
                escalation_type=(
                    EscalationType.PROVIDER_FAILURE
                ),
                severity=severity,
                provider=incident.provider,
                capability=capability,
                message=message,
                recommendation=(
                    recommendation
                ),
            )
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _determine_severity(
        incident: ProviderIncident,
    ) -> EscalationSeverity:
        """
        Determine outage escalation severity.

        Hackathon policy:

        - 3-4 consecutive failures:
          HIGH

        - 5+ consecutive failures:
          CRITICAL

        The policy is intentionally conservative:
        the system recommends escalation but never
        performs the operational action itself.
        """

        if incident.consecutive_failures >= 5:
            return EscalationSeverity.CRITICAL

        return EscalationSeverity.HIGH

    @staticmethod
    def _format_capability_scope(
        incident: ProviderIncident,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Represent the affected capability scope.
        """

        if not incident.affected_capabilities:
            return "provider-wide"

        return ", ".join(
            incident.affected_capabilities
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _build_message(
        incident: ProviderIncident,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Build an Operations Center escalation message.
        """

        capability_scope = (
            ProviderHealthEscalationService
            ._format_capability_scope(
                incident
            )
        )

        return (
            f"Provider {incident.provider} is "
            f"currently unavailable. "
            f"Affected capabilities: "
            f"{capability_scope}. "
            f"Consecutive health failures: "
            f"{incident.consecutive_failures}. "
            f"Incident started at "
            f"{incident.started_at.isoformat()}."
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _build_recommendation(
        incident: ProviderIncident,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Build an advisory recommendation.

        No routing change is executed.
        """

        capability_scope = (
            ProviderHealthEscalationService
            ._format_capability_scope(
                incident
            )
        )

        return (
            f"Review alternative provider options "
            f"for affected capabilities "
            f"({capability_scope}) and keep the "
            f"current provider change under human "
            f"approval until recovery is confirmed."
        )

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_health_models.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center provider health models.

Defines the domain contracts used to monitor AI service
provider availability and operational health.

Architecture:

Provider Health Probe
        |
        v
ProviderHealthObservation
        |
        v
ProviderHealthService
        |
        v
ProviderIncidentService
        |
        v
Escalation Service
        |
        v
AI Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- represent provider health states;
- represent individual health observations;
- represent provider outage information;
- represent health transitions;
- provide immutable domain contracts.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- perform health probes;
- persist data;
- send emails;
- modify Runtime routing;
- create autonomous failover.

Human approval remains mandatory for operational changes.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum


class ProviderHealthStatus(
    str,
    Enum,
):
    """
    Current operational health state of an AI provider.
    """

    HEALTHY = "healthy"

    DEGRADED = "degraded"

    UNAVAILABLE = "unavailable"

    UNKNOWN = "unknown"


class ProviderHealthTransition(
    str,
    Enum,
):
    """
    Significant transition in provider health.

    These transitions are useful for incident detection
    and escalation decisions.
    """

    RECOVERED = "recovered"

    DEGRADED = "degraded"

    UNAVAILABLE = "unavailable"

    UNKNOWN = "unknown"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderHealthObservation:
    """
    Immutable observation produced by a provider health
    probe or an equivalent Runtime health signal.

    An observation represents what was observed at one
    point in time.

    It is not itself an incident.
    """

    provider: str

    model: str | None

    capability: str | None

    status: ProviderHealthStatus

    observed_at: datetime

    latency_ms: int | None = None

    error_type: str | None = None

    error_message: str | None = None

    consecutive_failures: int = 0

    consecutive_successes: int = 0


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderHealthState:
    """
    Current calculated health state of a provider.

    This represents the operational state derived from
    one or more health observations.

    It does not represent historical incident storage.
    """

    provider: str

    status: ProviderHealthStatus

    checked_at: datetime

    consecutive_failures: int

    consecutive_successes: int

    last_success_at: datetime | None = None

    last_failure_at: datetime | None = None

    degraded_since: datetime | None = None

    unavailable_since: datetime | None = None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderIncident:
    """
    Provider availability incident.

    An incident represents a sustained operational problem,
    rather than an individual failed request.

    Example:

        OpenAI
        unavailable
        started 14:32
        recovered 14:51
        downtime 19 minutes
    """

    incident_id: str

    provider: str

    status: ProviderHealthStatus

    started_at: datetime

    last_observed_at: datetime

    recovered_at: datetime | None = None

    affected_capabilities: tuple[
        str,
        ...,
    ] = ()

    consecutive_failures: int = 0

    resolution_message: str | None = None

    @property
    def is_open(self) -> bool:
        """
        Return whether the provider incident remains open.
        """

        return self.recovered_at is None

    @property
    def downtime_seconds(self) -> float | None:
        """
        Return completed downtime in seconds.

        Returns None while the incident remains open because
        final downtime has not yet been established.
        """

        if self.recovered_at is None:
            return None

        return (
            self.recovered_at
            - self.started_at
        ).total_seconds()


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderHealthTransitionEvent:
    """
    Significant provider health transition.

    Used by application services to determine whether an
    incident should be created, updated, recovered, or
    escalated.
    """

    provider: str

    transition: ProviderHealthTransition

    previous_status: ProviderHealthStatus

    current_status: ProviderHealthStatus

    occurred_at: datetime

    consecutive_failures: int

    consecutive_successes: int

    affected_capabilities: tuple[
        str,
        ...,
    ] = ()

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_health_monitor_service.py
------------------------------------------------------------
"""
EasyChatBot AI Operations Center provider health monitor.

Coordinates provider health observations and their
read-only operational projections.

Architecture:

Provider Health Observation
        |
        v
ProviderHealthService
        |
        v
ProviderHealthMonitorService
        |
        v
ProviderHealthReadModel
        |
        v
Operations Center


Responsibilities:

- accept provider health observations;
- delegate health calculation to ProviderHealthService;
- retain the latest observation metadata;
- expose dashboard-safe provider health read models;
- expose all currently observed providers.

Does NOT:

- execute providers;
- perform health probes;
- persist incidents;
- send emails;
- change Runtime routing;
- approve recommendations;
- perform autonomous failover.

ProviderHealthService remains the authority for health
state calculation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_models import (
    ProviderHealthObservation,
    ProviderHealthState,
    ProviderHealthTransitionEvent,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_read_model import (
    ProviderHealthReadModel,
    ProviderHealthReadModelService,
)

from app.application.provider_management.provider_health_service import (
    ProviderHealthService,
)


class ProviderHealthMonitorService:
    """
    Coordinates provider health state and operational
    read-model generation.

    The service deliberately keeps the latest observation
    separately from calculated health state because the
    current ProviderHealthState contract does not contain
    model or capability metadata.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        health_service: ProviderHealthService | None = None,
        read_model_service: (
            ProviderHealthReadModelService | None
        ) = None,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the provider health monitor.
        """

        self._health_service = (
            health_service
            if health_service is not None
            else ProviderHealthService()
        )

        self._read_model_service = (
            read_model_service
            if read_model_service is not None
            else ProviderHealthReadModelService()
        )

        self._latest_observations: dict[
            str,
            ProviderHealthObservation,
        ] = {}

    def observe(
        self,
        observation: ProviderHealthObservation,
    ) -> ProviderHealthTransitionEvent:
        """
        Process a provider health observation.

        Health calculation remains delegated to
        ProviderHealthService.
        """

        if observation is None:
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider health observation cannot be None."
            )

        transition = self._health_service.observe(
            observation
        )

        self._latest_observations[
            observation.provider
        ] = observation

        return transition

    def get_provider_health(
        self,
        provider: str,
    ) -> ProviderHealthReadModel | None:
        """
        Return the current operational health projection
        for one provider.
        """

        if not provider.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Provider cannot be empty."
            )

        state = self._health_service.get_state(
            provider
        )

        observation = self._latest_observations.get(
            provider
        )

        if (
            state is None
            or observation is None
        ):
            return None

        return self._read_model_service.build(
            observation=observation,
            state=state,
        )

    def get_all_provider_health(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[
        ProviderHealthReadModel,
        ...
    ]:
        """
        Return operational health projections for all
        observed providers.

        Results are deterministic and sorted by provider.
        """

        results: list[
            ProviderHealthReadModel
        ] = []

        for state in (
            self._health_service.get_all_states()
        ):
            observation = (
                self._latest_observations.get(
                    state.provider
                )
            )

            if observation is None:
                continue

            results.append(
                self._read_model_service.build(
                    observation=observation,
                    state=state,
                )
            )

        return tuple(
            sorted(
                results,
                key=lambda item: item.provider,
            )
        )

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """
        Clear tracked health state and observations.

        Intended for isolated tests and demonstrations.
        """

        self._health_service.clear()

        self._latest_observations.clear()

FILE: C:\AI Folder\EasyChatBot-Hackathon\app\application\provider_management\provider_health_read_model.py
